It got to the point where it actually scared me, so I ended up telling my supervisor about it, and, well, here I am.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about your family being better off without you?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been about two weeks. This whole change in me... it really started right around the time my daughter left Georgia to join the military. That's when these thoughts started coming up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I usually like to mess around in the garden with my tomato plants, or spend time sanding wood at my workbench. I'd watch the Falcons games, or stay after church just talking with folks. But these past couple weeks... I don't know. I just look at all those things and it feels like they belong to somebody else. I just don't have the energy or the care for it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any pleasure in doing things over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, no, not really. I just don't get anything out of it anymore. The tomatoes, the woodworking, the football games, talking to people at church... I just look at it all and feel nothing. It's like none of it even matters to me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your movement or activity level over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's... well, it's like my whole body is just moving through mud. Even the folks at work have been asking if I'm coming down with something. They say I'm walking a lot slower than usual, and... I don't know, it takes me forever just to answer simple questions they ask me. Everything just feels heavy and slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Looking back on it... I guess these low stretches have been coming around for about a year now. But this one right here, the one that started up after my daughter left... it's definitely hit me the hardest.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the heaviness and slow movement, specifically?\n\nPatient: It's been about two weeks. This whole change in me... the heaviness and everything... it really started up right after my daughter left Georgia to go into the military. So, yeah, right around two weeks now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I, uh... I came in because I've been having some thoughts lately that really started to bother me. I just keep thinking that maybe my family would be better off if I just didn't wake up in the morning. It got to the point where it actually scared me, so I ended up telling my supervisor about it, and, well, here I am.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about your family being better off without you?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been about two weeks. This whole change in me... it really started right around the time my daughter left Georgia to join the military. That's when these thoughts started coming up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I usually like to mess around in the garden with my tomato plants, or spend time sanding wood at my workbench. I'd watch the Falcons games, or stay after church just talking with folks. But these past couple weeks... I don't know. I just look at all those things and it feels like they belong to somebody else. I just don't have the energy or the care for it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any pleasure in doing things over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, no, not really. I just don't get anything out of it anymore. The tomatoes, the woodworking, the football games, talking to people at church... I just look at it all and feel nothing. It's like none of it even matters to me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your movement or activity level over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's... well, it's like my whole body is just moving through mud. Even the folks at work have been asking if I'm coming down with something. They say I'm walking a lot slower than usual, and... I don't know, it takes me forever just to answer simple questions they ask me. Everything just feels heavy and slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Looking back on it... I guess these low stretches have been coming around for about a year now. But this one right here, the one that started up after my daughter left... it's definitely hit me the hardest.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the heaviness and slow movement, specifically?\n\nPatient: It's been about two weeks. This whole change in me... the heaviness and everything... it really started up right after my daughter left Georgia to go into the military. So, yeah, right around two weeks now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to do a good bit... mess with my tomato plants, sand wood out at my bench, watch the Falcons play... I'd even stay after church just talking with folks. But lately... I don't know, I just look at all that stuff and it feels like it belongs to somebody else. I just don't have any drive to do any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to feel happy or experience any kind of pleasure when good things happen to you or to those you care about?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. It's kind of like what I was just saying a second ago. Even if something good happens... like if the Falcons win a big game, or folks have good news down at church... I just don't feel it. I look at the things that used to make me smile, and it's just flat. Like I said, it just feels like those good feelings belong to somebody else right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of more than two months where you didn't feel this way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's been pretty steady like this for the last couple of weeks, since she left. Before that, I was doing fine, but right now... no, there hasn't been any break in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year where you felt completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's really just been these last couple of weeks that I've been feeling this low. Before that, for most of the past year, I was pretty much my normal self. So, yeah, I guess you could say I felt fine before all this started recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more years where you felt this low?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. Like I was just saying, it's really only been these last couple of weeks. I've never had a time where I felt this down for years on end.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I just keep thinking that maybe my family would be better off if I just didn't wake up in the morning. It got to the point where it actually scared me, so I ended up telling my supervisor about it, and, well, here I am.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about your family being better off without you?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been about two weeks. This whole change in me... it really started right around the time my daughter left Georgia to join the military. That's when these thoughts started coming up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I usually like to mess around in the garden with my tomato plants, or spend time sanding wood at my workbench. I'd watch the Falcons games, or stay after church just talking with folks. But these past couple weeks... I don't know. I just look at all those things and it feels like they belong to somebody else. I just don't have the energy or the care for it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any pleasure in doing things over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, no, not really. I just don't get anything out of it anymore. The tomatoes, the woodworking, the football games, talking to people at church... I just look at it all and feel nothing. It's like none of it even matters to me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your movement or activity level over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I don't know, it takes me forever just to answer simple questions they ask me. Everything just feels heavy and slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I used to do a good bit... mess with my tomato plants, sand wood out at my bench, watch the Falcons play... I'd even stay after church just talking with folks. But lately... I don't know, I just look at all that stuff and it feels like it belongs to somebody else. I just don't have any drive to do any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to feel happy or experience any kind of pleasure when good things happen to you or to those you care about?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. It's kind of like what I was just saying a second ago. Even if something good happens... like if the Falcons win a big game, or folks have good news down at church... I just don't feel it. I look at the things that used to make me smile, and it's just flat. Like I said, it just feels like those good feelings belong to somebody else right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of more than two months where you didn't feel this way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's been pretty steady like this for the last couple of weeks, since she left. Before that, I was doing fine, but right now... no, there hasn't been any break in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year where you felt completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's really just been these last couple of weeks that I've been feeling this low. Before that, for most of the past year, I was pretty much my normal self. So, yeah, I guess you could say I felt fine before all this started recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more years where you felt this low?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. Like I was just saying, it's really only been these last couple of weeks. I've never had a time where I felt this down for years on end.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling low like this for two years or more at any point in your life?\n\nPatient: No, never. I just don't have the energy or the care for it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any pleasure in doing things over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, no, not really.
